\Upsilon_{(S,S^{\prime})}(t)\leq M_{\vec{\mathcal{S}}}(t)

for any
t\in[0,2]
.
Minimizing equation (
7.7
) over all

\vec{\mathcal{S}}\in P_{\st}(S,S^{\prime})
yields the result.
∎

8.
The summand-swapping diffeomorphism

If
K
is a knot in
\mathbb{S}^{3}
, one can construct an order
n
automorphism of the knot

K^{\#n}
, corresponding to cyclically permuting the summands. In this
section, we investigate the case when
n=2
, and compute the induced map on
knot Floer homology. We will use this in Section
10
to construct
pairs of slice disks for which we can explicitly compute the secondary invariants
defined in Section
4
.

8.1.
Construction of the diffeomorphism map

If
K\subseteq\mathbb{S}^{3}
is a knot, there is a diffeomorphism

R^{\pi}\colon(\mathbb{S}^{3},K\#K)\to(\mathbb{S}^{3},K\#K)

that switches the two summands of
K\#K
. In fact, for an appropriate embedding of

K\#K
into
\mathbb{S}^{3}
, the diffeomorphism
R^{\pi}
can be realized as
an order 2 rigid motion of
\mathbb{S}^{3}
: Isotope
K
into the
y\geq 1
half-space of
\mathbb{R}^{3}\subseteq\mathbb{S}^{3}=\mathbb{R}^{3}\cup\{\infty\}

such that the line segment
I:=[-1,1]\times\{(1,0)\}\subseteq K
. For
\varphi\in\mathbb{R}
,
let
R^{\varphi}
be
\varphi
-rotation about the
z
-axis. If we let
K\#K
be the equivariant smoothing of

(K\setminus I)\cup R^{\pi}(K\setminus I)\cup R^{\pi/2}(I)\cup R^{-\pi/2}(I),

then
R^{\pi}
is an orientation-preserving automorphism of
(\mathbb{S}^{3},K\#K)
.

In particular, the knot
K\#K
is 2-periodic, and has no fixed points.
We pick two basepoints,
w
,
z\in K\setminus I
, such that
w
follows
z

with respect to the orientation of
K
. We let
w^{\prime}
and
z^{\prime}
denote their images
on
R^{\pi}(K\setminus I)
under the map
R^{\pi}
. We write
\mathbb{K}=(K,w,z)

and
\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K}=(K\#K,w,z)
.

We define the element

\bm{\mathbf{R}}^{\pi}:=\rho\circ R^{\pi}\in\MCG(\mathbb{S}^{3},K\#K,w,z),

where

\rho\colon(\mathbb{S}^{3},K\#K,w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\to(\mathbb{S}^{3},K\#K,w,z)

is a half-twist diffeomorphism in the direction of the knot’s orientation
that swaps the pairs
(w,z)
and
(w^{\prime},z^{\prime})
.
We note that the diffeomorphism
(\mathbf{R}^{\pi})^{2}
is isotopic to a full Dehn twist along
K\#K
.
Hence, by
[
54
]
*Theorem B,

(8.1)

(\bm{\mathbf{R}}^{\pi}_{*})^{2}\simeq\id+\Phi_{w}^{\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K}}\circ\Psi_{z}^{\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K}},

where
\Phi_{w}^{\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K}}
and
\Psi_{z}^{\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K}}
are the basepoint actions on
\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K})

described in Section
3.2
; see also
the work of Sarkar
[
47
]
.

Remark 8.1
.

Note that any
\mathbb{F}[U,V]
-equivariant homotopy equivalence

\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K})\simeq\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})\otimes\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})

will intertwine
\Phi_{w}^{\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K}}
(resp.
\Psi_{z}^{\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K}}
) with

\Phi_{w}\otimes\id+\id\otimes\Phi_{w}
(resp.
\Psi_{z}\otimes\id+\id\otimes\Psi_{z}
), up to chain homotopy. This is because if
C
and
C^{\prime}
are free chain complexes over
\mathbb{F}[U,V]
and
F\colon C\to C^{\prime}
is a chain map, one easily shows that
F\circ\Phi\simeq\Phi^{\prime}\circ F
, where
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
are the algebraic analogs of the map
\Phi_{w}
on
C
and
C^{\prime}
.

We now wish to compute a formula for the chain homotopy type of the induced
map
\bm{\mathbf{R}}^{\pi}_{*}
. Note that there is a filtered chain map

\Sw\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})\otimes\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})\otimes\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K}),

obtained by switching the two factors. Note that
\Sw
cannot be chain
homotopic to
\bm{\mathbf{R}}^{\pi}
, since
\Sw\circ\Sw=\id
, which would violate
equation (
8.1
). In this section, we prove the following:

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
\mathbb{K}=(K,w,z)
be a doubly-based knot in
\mathbb{S}^{3}
, and
consider the doubly-based knot
\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K}=(K\#K,w,z)
defined above. Then there is a
filtered chain homotopy equivalence between
\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K})
and

\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\infty}}\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})
that intertwines
\bm{\mathbf{R}}^{\pi}_{*}
with

\Sw\circ\left(\id\otimes\id+\id\otimes(\Phi_{w}\circ\Psi_{z})+\Psi_{z}\otimes\Phi_{w}\right).

Remark 8.3
.

We say the endomorphisms
F
and
G
of a chain complex
C
are

homotopy conjugate
if there is a homotopy automorphism

A\colon C\to C
such that
F\circ A\simeq A\circ G
.
It is not hard to see that the four maps

\begin{split}&\Sw\circ\left(\id\otimes\id+\id\otimes(\Phi_{w}\circ\Psi_{z})+\Psi_{z}\otimes\Phi_{w}\right),\\
&\Sw\circ\left(\id\otimes\id+\id\otimes(\Phi_{w}\circ\Psi_{z})+\Phi_{w}\otimes\Psi_{z}\right),\\
&\Sw\circ\left(\id\otimes\id+(\Phi_{w}\circ\Psi_{z})\otimes\id+\Psi_{z}\otimes\Phi_{w}\right),\\
&\Sw\circ\left(\id\otimes\id+(\Phi_{w}\circ\Psi_{z})\otimes\id+\Phi_{w}\otimes\Psi_{z}\right)\end{split}

are all homotopy conjugate endomorphisms of
\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})\otimes\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})
.
Indeed, the map
A
can be taken to be one of the maps
\Sw
,
\id\otimes\id+\Phi_{w}\otimes\Psi_{z}
,
or
\id\otimes\id+\Psi_{z}\otimes\Phi_{w}
,
since
\Phi^{2}_{w}\simeq 0
,
\Psi^{2}_{z}\simeq 0
, and
\Phi_{w}\circ\Psi_{z}\simeq\Psi_{z}\circ\Phi_{w}
.

8.2.
Proof of the formula for the summand-swapping diffeomorphism map

Proof of Theorem
8.2
.

Let us write
\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K}=(K\#K,w,z)
, where
w
and
z
appear on the right copy of
K
.
Let
w^{\prime}
and
z^{\prime}
denote their images under
R^{\pi}
, on the left copy of
K
.
We define our connected sum map

E\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K})\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(K,w^{\prime},z^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\infty}}\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathbb{K})

as the composition

E:=F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+},

where
F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}
denotes the 3-handle map induced by the framed 2-sphere
\mathbb{S}_{2}

that separates the two copies of
K
after the band surgery along
B
.
A schematic of the link cobordism corresponding to
E

is shown in Figure
8.1
.

Figure 8.1.
A schematic of the map
E:=F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}
.
A decomposition of the surface is shown, corresponding to the factors of
F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}
and
T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}
in
E
.
The 3-handle map
F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}
is not shown.

The map
E
is a chain homotopy equivalence. Indeed,
E
is the map
induced by a pair-of-pants link cobordism in a 3-handle cobordism
that is diffeomorphic to the reverse of one of the two connected sum
cobordisms constructed in
[
56
]
*Section 5
(in fact, it is diffeomorphic to the link cobordism inducing the map
E_{1}
, described therein).
According to
[
58
]
*Proposition 5.1, the map
E
is a chain homotopy equivalence,
and a homotopy inverse is given by turning around and reversing the orientation of the cobordism.

Expanding the definitions of
E
and
\bm{\mathbf{R}}^{\pi}
, and observing that
\rho\circ R^{\pi}
and

R^{\pi}\circ\rho
are equal as automorphisms of
(S^{3},\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K})
,
we have

(8.2)

E\bm{\mathbf{R}}^{\pi}_{*}\simeq F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}R^{\pi}_{*}\rho_{*}.

We note that, for the doubly-based knot
\rho(\mathbb{K}\#\mathbb{K})=(K\#K,w^{\prime},z^{\prime})
, we have

(8.3)

T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}R^{\pi}_{*}\simeq R^{\pi}_{*}T_{w,z}^{+}

by the functoriality of the quasi-stabilization operation. Similarly,

(8.4)

F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}R^{\pi}_{*}\simeq R^{\pi}_{*}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}},

since the diffeomorphism
R^{\pi}
preserves the connected sum band
B
setwise.
Finally, we note that

(8.5)

F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}R^{\pi}_{*}\simeq\Sw F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}},

since the framed sphere
\mathbb{S}_{2}
is fixed setwise by
R^{\pi}
.
We remark that, in equation (
8.5
),

R^{\pi}
reverses the orientation of the framed 2-sphere
\mathbb{S}_{2}
,
though this has no effect on the cobordism map.

Applying the relations from equations (
8.3
), (
8.4
),
and (
8.5
) to equation (
8.2
) yields

(8.6)

E\bm{\mathbf{R}}^{\pi}_{*}\simeq\Sw F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w,z}^{+}\rho_{*}.

Next, we examine the expression
F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w,z}^{+}\rho_{*}

appearing in equation (
8.6
). We perform the following manipulations:

(8.7)

\displaystyle F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w,z}^{+}\rho_{*}

\displaystyle\simeq F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w,z}^{+}S_{w,z}^{-}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}

\displaystyle(\text{equation}~\eqref{eq:R5})

\displaystyle\simeq F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}+F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}S_{w,z}^{+}T_{w,z}^{-}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}

\displaystyle(\text{equation}~\eqref{eq:R6}).

Next, we compute

(8.8)

\displaystyle F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}S_{w,z}^{+}T_{w,z}^{-}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}

\displaystyle\simeq F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}S_{w,z}^{+}S_{w,z}^{-}\Psi_{z}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}

\displaystyle(\text{equation}~\eqref{eq:R2})

\displaystyle\simeq F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}\Phi_{w}\Psi_{z}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}

\displaystyle(\text{equation}~\eqref{eq:R3}).

We note that
\Psi_{z^{\prime}}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}\simeq 0
,
since
T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}\simeq\Psi_{z^{\prime}}S_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}
and
\Psi_{z^{\prime}}^{2}\simeq 0

by equations (
3.6
) and (
3.3
). Hence

(8.9)

F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}\Phi_{w}\Psi_{z}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}\simeq F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}\Phi_{w}(\Psi_{z}+\Psi_{z^{\prime}})T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}.

From equations (
3.13
) and (
3.15
), we conclude that

(8.10)

F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}\Phi_{w}(\Psi_{z}+\Psi_{z^{\prime}})T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}\simeq F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}\Phi_{w}(\Psi_{z}+\Psi_{z^{\prime}})F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}.

Finally, we note that
\Phi_{w}
,
\Psi_{z}
, and
\Psi_{z^{\prime}}

commute with
F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}
by
[
58
]
*Lemma 8.3, hence

(8.11)

F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}\Phi_{w}(\Psi_{z}+\Psi_{z^{\prime}})F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}\simeq(\id\otimes(\Phi_{w}\Psi_{z})+\Psi_{z^{\prime}}\otimes\Phi_{w})F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}.

Combining, equations (
8.7
)–(
8.11
),
and using the fact that
E:=F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w^{\prime},z^{\prime}}^{+}
, we see that

(8.12)

F_{\mathbb{S}_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w,z}^{+}\rho_{*}\simeq(\id\otimes\id+\id\otimes\Phi_{w}\Psi_{z}+\Psi_{z^{\prime}}\otimes\Phi_{w})E.

By applying
\Sw
to equation (
8.12
), and
combining it with equation (
8.6
), we obtain that

E\bm{\mathbf{R}}^{\pi}_{*}\simeq\Sw(\id\otimes\id+\id\otimes\Phi_{w}\Psi_{z}+\Psi_{z^{\prime}}\otimes\Phi_{w})E.

Upon relabeling
w^{\prime}
and
z^{\prime}
as
w
and
z
, we obtain the formula in the statement.
∎

9.
The trace formula

If
(Y,\mathbb{L})
is a multi-based link, the identity decorated link cobordism

(W_{\id},\mathcal{F}_{\id})\colon(Y,\mathbb{L})\to(Y,\mathbb{L})

is constructed by decorating
(I\times Y,I\times L)
with a dividing set

\mathcal{A}=I\times\bm{\mathbf{p}}
, where
\bm{\mathbf{p}}\subseteq L
consists of one point in each
component of
L\setminus(\bm{\mathbf{w}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{z}})
.

By changing which ends of
(W_{\id},\mathcal{F}_{\id})
are designated as incoming
or outgoing, we get two other decorated link cobordisms, which we denote by

(W_{\tr},\mathcal{F}_{\tr})\colon(-Y\sqcup Y,-\mathbb{L}\sqcup\mathbb{L})\to\emptyset\quad\text{and}\quad(W_{\cotr},\mathcal{F}_{\cotr})\colon\emptyset\to(Y\sqcup-Y,\mathbb{L}\sqcup-\mathbb{L}).

The
\mathcal{R}
-module
\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(-Y,-\mathbb{L},\mathfrak{s})
is canonically isomorphic to

\Hom_{\mathcal{R}^{\infty}}(\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(Y,\mathbb{L},\mathfrak{s}),\mathcal{R}^{\infty})
, and hence there is
a
canonical trace pairing

\tr\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(-Y,-\mathbb{L},\mathfrak{s})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\infty}}\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(Y,\mathbb{L},\mathfrak{s})\to\mathcal{R}^{\infty}.

Similarly there is a canonical cotrace map

\cotr\colon\mathcal{R}^{\infty}\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(Y,\mathbb{L},\mathfrak{s})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\infty}}\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(-Y,-\mathbb{L},\mathfrak{s}),

obtained by dualizing the trace pairing.
In this section, we prove the following:

Theorem 9.1
.

The trace and cotrace cobordisms induce the canonical trace and cotrace maps:

F_{W_{\tr},\mathcal{F}_{\tr},\mathfrak{s}}\simeq\tr\quad\text{and}\quad F_{W_{\cotr},\mathcal{F}_{\cotr},\mathfrak{s}}\simeq\cotr.

Our proof of Theorem
9.1
is similar to the proofs of

[
55
]
*Theorem 1.6 and

[
20
]
*Theorem 1.2.

9.1.
Heegaard triples and link cobordisms

Definition 9.2
.

We say that
(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a
Heegaard link triple

if
(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}})
is a Heegaard triple diagram decorated with two disjoint
collections of basepoints,
\bm{\mathbf{w}}
and
\bm{\mathbf{z}}
. Furthermore, for each
\bm{\mathbf{\sigma}}\in\{\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}\}
, each component of
\Sigma\setminus\bm{\mathbf{\sigma}}
is planar, and
contains exactly one
\bm{\mathbf{w}}
basepoint, and exactly one
\bm{\mathbf{z}}
basepoint.

We remark that such a Heegaard triple is called a
doubly multi-pointed Heegaard triple
in
[
58
]
.
If
(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a Heegaard link triple
and
\bm{\mathbf{\sigma}},\bm{\mathbf{\eta}}\in\{\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}\}
, then we write
(Y_{\sigma,\eta},\mathbb{L}_{\sigma,\eta})

for the multi-based link defined by the diagram
(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\sigma}},\bm{\mathbf{\eta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
.
There is a decorated link cobordism

(X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma})\colon(Y_{\alpha,\beta}\sqcup Y_{\beta,\gamma},\mathbb{L}_{\alpha,\beta}\sqcup\mathbb{L}_{\beta,\gamma})\to(Y_{\alpha,\gamma},\mathbb{L}_{\alpha,\gamma}),

described in
[
20
]
*Section 9.4, which is a
refinement of the construction from
[
39
]
*Section 8.1. The
4-manifold
X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
is constructed as the union

X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}:=(\Delta\times\Sigma)\cup(e_{\alpha}\times U_{\alpha})\cup(e_{\beta}\times U_{\beta})\cup(e_{\gamma}\times U_{\gamma}),

where
U_{\alpha},

U_{\beta}
, and
U_{\gamma}
denote handlebodies with boundary

\Sigma
, with compressing curves
\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}
,
\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}
, and
\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}
, respectively.

The decorated surface
\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}=(S_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathcal{A}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma})
is constructed as follows.
We pick embedded paths in
\Sigma\setminus\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}
,
\Sigma\setminus\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}
, and

\Sigma\setminus\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}
connecting the
\bm{\mathbf{z}}
-basepoints to the

\bm{\mathbf{w}}
-basepoints, and then push the interiors of these arcs into the
interior of
U_{\alpha}
,
U_{\beta}
, or
U_{\gamma}
, respectively.
We obtain three sets of properly embedded arcs

\ell_{\alpha}\subseteq U_{\alpha}
,
\ell_{\beta}\subseteq U_{\beta}
, and
\ell_{\gamma}\subseteq U_{\gamma}
.
The surface
S_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
is defined as

S_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}:=(\Delta\times(\bm{\mathbf{w}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{z}}))\cup(e_{\alpha}\times\ell_{\alpha})\cup(e_{\beta}\times\ell_{\beta})\cup(e_{\gamma}\times\ell_{\gamma}).

To obtain
\mathcal{A}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
, choose subsets
\bm{\mathbf{p}}_{\alpha}\subseteq\ell_{\alpha}
,

\bm{\mathbf{p}}_{\beta}\subseteq\ell_{\beta}
, and
\bm{\mathbf{p}}_{\gamma}\subseteq\ell_{\gamma}
consisting of
one point in the interior of each component of
\ell_{\alpha}
,
\ell_{\beta}
, and

\ell_{\gamma}
, and set

\mathcal{A}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}:=(e_{\alpha}\times\bm{\mathbf{p}}_{\alpha})\cup(e_{\beta}\times\bm{\mathbf{p}}_{\beta})\cup(e_{\gamma}\times\bm{\mathbf{p}}_{\gamma}).

Theorem 9.3
.

If
(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a Heegaard link triple, then the
cobordism map
F_{W_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathfrak{s}}
is chain homotopic to
the holomorphic triangle map

F_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\mathfrak{s}}\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}},\mathfrak{s}|_{Y_{\alpha,\beta}})\otimes\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}},\mathfrak{s}|_{Y_{\beta,\gamma}})\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}},\mathfrak{s}|_{Y_{\alpha,\gamma}}),

defined by the formula

F_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\mathfrak{s}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\bm{\mathbf{y}}):=\sum_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\alpha}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\psi\in\pi_{2}(\bm{\mathbf{x}},\bm{\mathbf{y}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})\\
\mu(\psi)=0\\
\mathfrak{s}_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\psi)=\mathfrak{s}\end{subarray}}\#\mathcal{M}(\psi)\cdot U^{n_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\psi)}V^{n_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\psi)}\cdot\bm{\mathbf{z}}

for
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{T}_{\alpha}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\beta}
and
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{T}_{\beta}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
.

We now demonstrate that the trace formula follows quickly from Theorem
9.3
:

Proof of Theorem
9.1
.

We will focus on the claim that
F_{W_{\tr},\mathcal{F}_{\tr},\mathfrak{s}}\simeq\tr
. The
claim about the cotrace cobordism follows from the formula for the trace
cobordism. Indeed, if
(W,\mathcal{F})
is a decorated link cobordism from

(Y_{1},\mathbb{L}_{1})
to
(Y_{2},\mathbb{L}_{2})
, and

(W^{\vee},\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\colon(-Y_{2},-\mathbb{L}_{2})\to(-Y_{1},-\mathbb{L}_{1})

is the cobordism obtained by turning around
(W,\mathcal{F})
, then it is
straightforward to adapt the proof of
[
40
]
*Theorem 3.5 to see
that
F_{W^{\vee},\mathcal{F}^{\vee},\mathfrak{s}}
is equal to the dual map

(F_{W,\mathcal{F},\mathfrak{s}})^{\vee}\colon\Hom_{\mathcal{R}^{\infty}}(\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(Y_{2},\mathbb{L}_{2},\mathfrak{s}|_{Y_{2}}),\mathcal{R}^{\infty})\to\Hom_{\mathcal{R}^{\infty}}(\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(Y_{1},\mathbb{L}_{1},\mathfrak{s}|_{Y_{1}}),\mathcal{R}^{\infty}).

To establish the formula for
F_{W_{\tr},\mathcal{F}_{\tr},\mathfrak{s}}
, we pick a diagram

(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
for
(Y,\mathbb{L})
, as well as a small Hamiltonian
translate
\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}^{\prime}
of
\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}
, and we consider the Heegaard triple

(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}^{\prime},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
. The decorated link cobordism

(X_{\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta},\mathcal{F}_{\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta})
is in fact equal to
(I\times Y,I\times L)

with a neighborhood of
\{\tfrac{1}{2}\}\times U_{\beta}
removed. Hence, using
Theorem
9.3
and the composition law, we can write

(9.1)

F_{W_{\tr},\mathcal{F}_{\tr},\mathfrak{s}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\bm{\mathbf{y}})=(G\circ F_{\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta,\mathfrak{s}})(\bm{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\bm{\mathbf{y}}),

where
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{T}_{\beta^{\prime}}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\alpha}
and
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{T}_{\alpha}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\beta}
,
and
G
is a composition of 3-handle and 4-handle maps. The map
G
takes the form

G(\Theta)=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }\Theta=\Theta_{\beta^{\prime},\beta}^{-},\\
0&\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

for
\Theta\in\mathbb{T}_{\beta^{\prime}}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\beta}
, and extended
\mathcal{R}^{\infty}
-equivariantly. On the other hand
equation (
9.1
) says that

F_{W_{\tr},\mathcal{F}_{\tr},\mathfrak{s}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\bm{\mathbf{y}})
is exactly equal to the count of Maslov index 0
holomorphic triangles with vertices
\bm{\mathbf{x}}
,
\bm{\mathbf{y}}
, and

\Theta_{\beta^{\prime},\beta}^{-}
. Note that
\Theta_{\beta^{\prime},\beta}^{-}=\Theta_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}^{+}
, and that
the transition map

\Phi_{\beta\to\beta^{\prime}}^{\alpha}\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}},\mathfrak{s})\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}^{\prime},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}},\mathfrak{s})

can be computed via the triangle
map
F_{\alpha,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}}(-\otimes\Theta_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}^{+})
. Observing that

F_{\alpha,\beta,\beta^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}}
and
F_{\beta^{\prime},\alpha,\beta,\mathfrak{s}}
count the exact same
holomorphic triangles, we conclude that

F_{W_{\tr},\mathcal{F}_{\tr},\mathfrak{s}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\bm{\mathbf{y}})=\tr(\bm{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\Phi_{\beta\to\beta^{\prime}}^{\alpha}(\bm{\mathbf{y}})),

completing the proof.
∎

9.2.
Compound 1- and 3-handle maps and some related counts of holomorphic curves

Suppose that
(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
and

(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})
are two multi-pointed Heegaard diagrams,
and that we have a choice of injection

i\colon\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\to\bm{\mathbf{z}}.

Suppose further that
(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})
satisfies the following:

(
D1
)

The curves
\bm{\mathbf{\xi}}
can be related to the curves
\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}}
by a sequence of
isotopies and handleslides
in the complement of
\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}
and
\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0}
.

(
D2
)

Each
\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}
-basepoint is contained in the same region of

\Sigma_{0}\setminus(\bm{\mathbf{\xi}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}})
as a
\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0}
-basepoint.

(
D3
)

|\xi_{i}\cap\zeta_{j}|=2\delta_{ij}
, and
\xi_{i}\cap\zeta_{i}
consists of
two points that have relative Maslov grading 1.

Note that condition (
D1
) implies that

(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})
is a diagram for an unlink
\mathbb{U}
in

(\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})^{\#g(\Sigma_{0})}
, each of whose components
contains exactly two basepoints. Condition (
D2
) implies
that
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}})=\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}})
for any intersection point
\bm{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\xi}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\zeta}
. Finally, condition (
D3
)
implies that there is a top-graded intersection point

\Theta^{+}_{\xi,\zeta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\xi}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\zeta}
, and a bottom-graded
intersection point
\Theta^{-}_{\xi,\zeta}\in\mathbb{T}_{\xi}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\zeta}
.

We form the surface
\Sigma\#_{i}\Sigma_{0}
by joining
\Sigma
and
\Sigma_{0}

together with a connected sum tube for each point
w_{0}\in\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}
, which is
attached near the points
w_{0}
and
i(w_{0})
. Let us write

\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}:=(\bm{\mathbf{z}}\setminus i(\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}))\cup\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0}.

There is an induced Heegaard diagram
(\Sigma\#_{i}\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime})
.

We define the
compound 1-handle map

F_{1}^{\xi,\zeta}\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}_{J_{s}}(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}_{J_{s}(\bm{\mathbf{T}})}(\Sigma\#_{i}\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime})

via the formula

F_{1}^{\xi,\zeta}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}):=\bm{\mathbf{x}}\times\Theta^{+}_{\xi,\zeta}

for
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{T}_{\alpha}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\beta}
, and extended
\mathcal{R}^{\infty}
-equivariantly.
Here
J_{s}
and
J_{s}(\bm{\mathbf{T}})
are 1-parameter families of almost complex
structures on
\Sigma\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
and
(\Sigma\#_{i}\Sigma_{0})\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
,
respectively, that we will describe shortly.

Similarly there is a
compound 3-handle map

F_{3}^{\xi,\zeta}\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}_{J_{s}(\bm{\mathbf{T}})}(\Sigma\#_{i}\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}_{J_{s}}(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}}),

defined via the formula

F_{3}^{\xi,\zeta}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}\times\Theta):=\begin{cases}\bm{\mathbf{x}}&\text{ if }\Theta=\Theta^{-}_{\xi,\zeta},\\
0&\text{ if }\Theta\neq\Theta^{-}_{\xi,\zeta}\end{cases}

for
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{T}_{\alpha}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\beta}
and
\Theta\in\mathbb{T}_{\xi}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\zeta}
, and extended
\mathcal{R}^{\infty}
-equivariantly.

We now wish to show that the compound 1-handle and 3-handle maps are chain
maps. This involves an argument involving analyzing how holomorphic curves
behave as one degenerates the almost complex structure. Lipshitz’s
cylindrical reformation of Heegaard Floer homology
[
25
]

provides the technical framework necessary to perform the analysis. Let us
write
n=|\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}|
, the number of connected sum tubes we add. Given almost
complex structures
J_{s}
and
J_{s}^{\prime}
on
\Sigma\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
and
\Sigma_{0}\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
that are split in a neighborhood of the connected sum
points, as well as an
n
-tuple of positive numbers
\bm{\mathbf{T}}=(T_{1},\dots,T_{n})
, we can form an almost complex structure
J_{s}(\bm{\mathbf{T}})
on
(\Sigma\#_{i}\Sigma_{0})\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
by inserting necks of length
T_{1},\dots,T_{n}

along the connected sum tubes.

Proposition 9.4
.

Suppose that
(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})
is a Heegaard diagram
satisfying conditions (
D1
), (
D2
), and
(
D3
). If
\bm{\mathbf{T}}
is an
n
-tuple of neck lengths, all of
whose components are sufficiently large, the compound 1-handle map

F_{1}^{\xi,\zeta}
and the compound 3-handle map
F_{3}^{\xi,\zeta}
are chain
maps.

Proposition
9.4
follows from a careful
analysis of how holomorphic curves in
(\Sigma\#_{i}\Sigma_{0})\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}
degenerate as one sends all components of
\bm{\mathbf{T}}
to
+\infty
,
simultaneously. The technical details of the proof can be found in

[
55
]
*Proposition 6.1.

There is an analogue of Proposition
9.4
for
the holomorphic triangle maps, which we will need for our proof of the trace
formula. Suppose that
(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{\tau}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})
is a
Heegaard link triple satisfying the following:

(
T
)

All three sub-diagrams of
(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{\tau}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})
satisfy conditions (
D1
), (
D2
), and (
D3
).

Note that condition (
T
) implies that there are
top-graded intersection points
\Theta_{\xi,\zeta}^{+}
,
\Theta_{\xi,\tau}^{+}
,
and
\Theta_{\zeta,\tau}^{+}
, as well as bottom-graded intersection
points
\Theta_{\xi,\zeta}^{-}
,
\Theta_{\xi,\tau}^{-}
, and
\Theta_{\zeta,\tau}^{-}
.

Suppose
\mathcal{T}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
and

\mathcal{T}_{0}=(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{\tau}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})
are Heegaard link triples
such that the latter satisfies condition (
T
) above,
and that we have a fixed injection

i\colon\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\to\bm{\mathbf{z}}.

We can construct a surface
\Sigma\#_{i}\Sigma_{0}
as we did before, as well as a Heegaard link triple

\mathcal{T}\#\mathcal{T}_{0}:=(\Sigma\#_{i}\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\tau}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}),

where
\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}:=(\bm{\mathbf{z}}\setminus i(\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}))\cup\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0}
.

Using a Mayer–Vietoris argument, it is not hard to see that there is an isomorphism

\Spin^{c}(X_{\alpha\cup\xi,\beta\cup\zeta,\gamma\cup\tau})\cong\Spin^{c}(X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma})\times\Spin^{c}(X_{\xi,\zeta,\tau}),

under which
\mathfrak{s}_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}}(\psi\#\psi_{0})
is identified with

(\mathfrak{s}_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\psi),\mathfrak{s}_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}}(\psi_{0}))
. For triples

(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{\tau}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})
satisfying
condition (
T
), the 4-manifold
X_{\xi,\zeta,\tau}

becomes
\#^{g(\Sigma_{0})}(\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{S}^{3})
once we glue in 3- and
4-handles along the boundary. In particular, there is a unique
\Spin^{c}

structure
\mathfrak{s}_{0}\in\Spin^{c}(X_{\xi,\zeta,\tau})
which restricts to the
torsion
\Spin^{c}
structure on all three boundary components. If
\mathfrak{s}\in\Spin^{c}(X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma})
, there is thus a well-defined
\Spin^{c}
structure

\mathfrak{s}\#\mathfrak{s}_{0}\in\Spin^{c}(X_{\alpha\cup\xi,\beta\cup\zeta,\gamma\cup\tau})
.

The holomorphic triangle counts from

[
55
]
*Proposition 6.3 carry over to our present
situation without change to imply the following:

Proposition 9.5
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{T}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
and

\mathcal{T}_{0}=(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{\tau}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})
are Heegaard link triples
such that the latter satisfies condition (
T
), and
i\colon\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\to\bm{\mathbf{z}}

is a chosen injection. Let
\mathcal{T}\#\mathcal{T}_{0}
denote the Heegaard link triple described
above. Then, for a tuple of sufficiently large neck-lengths
\bm{\mathbf{T}}
, the
following hold:

\displaystyle F_{\mathcal{T}\#\mathcal{T}_{0},J(\bm{\mathbf{T}}),\mathfrak{s}\#\mathfrak{s}_{0}}(F_{1}^{\xi,\zeta}(-)\otimes F_{1}^{\zeta,\tau}(-))

\displaystyle=F_{1}^{\xi,\tau}F_{\mathcal{T},J,\mathfrak{s}}(-\otimes-),

\displaystyle F_{3}^{\xi,\tau}F_{\mathcal{T}\#\mathcal{T}_{0},J(\bm{\mathbf{T}}),\mathfrak{s}\#\mathfrak{s}_{0}}(F_{1}^{\xi,\zeta}(-)\otimes-)

\displaystyle=F_{\mathcal{T},J,\mathfrak{s}}(-\otimes F_{3}^{\zeta,\tau}(-)),

\displaystyle F_{3}^{\xi,\tau}F_{\mathcal{T}\#\mathcal{T}_{0},J(\bm{\mathbf{T}}),\mathfrak{s}\#\mathfrak{s}_{0}}(-\otimes F_{1}^{\zeta,\tau}(-))

\displaystyle=F_{\mathcal{T},J,\mathfrak{s}}(F_{3}^{\xi,\zeta}(-)\otimes-).

Remark 9.6
.

Consider the special case when

(\Sigma_{0},\bm{\mathbf{\xi}},\bm{\mathbf{\zeta}},\bm{\mathbf{\tau}},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{0},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{0})=(\mathbb{S}^{2},\xi,\zeta,\tau,\{w_{0},w_{0}^{\prime}\},\{z_{0},z_{0}^{\prime}\})

is a Heegaard triple where
w_{0}
and
z_{0}
, and also
w_{0}^{\prime}
and
z_{0}^{\prime}

are adjacent, and where any two of
\xi
,
\zeta
, and
\tau

intersect in two points. Then Propositions
9.4

and
9.5
imply more standard relations between the
holomorphic disk and triangle counts, and the 1-handle and 3-handle
maps, for 1-handles with feet attached near the
\bm{\mathbf{z}}
basepoints on the
original Heegaard diagram. Compare
[
40
]
*Theorem 2.14 and

[
53
]
*Lemma 8.5 and Theorem 8.8.

Finally, we need an additional holomorphic triangle count, due to Manolescu
and Ozsváth
[
28
]
*Proposition 6.2,
which is useful in the
proof that the quasi-stabilization maps are well defined. Suppose that

\mathcal{T}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a Heegaard triple, and write
A
for a
distinguished component of
\Sigma\setminus\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}
. Let
w
and
z
be the two
basepoints in
A
. Suppose
\alpha_{s}
is a simple closed curve in
A
that
divides
A
into two components, one of which contains
w
, and the other
contains
z
. Let
p\in\alpha_{s}\setminus(\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}})
be an arbitrary
choice of point. We form the quasi-stabilized Heegaard triple
\mathcal{T}^{+}
via the
formula

\mathcal{T}^{+}:=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\{\alpha_{s}\},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\{\beta_{0}\},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}\cup\{\gamma_{0}\},\bm{\mathbf{w}}\cup\{w_{0}\},\bm{\mathbf{z}}\cup\{z_{0}\}),

where
\alpha_{s},

\beta_{0}
,
\gamma_{0},

w_{0}
, and
z_{0}
are as shown in
Figure
9.1
. (Compare Figure
3.2
). The curves
\beta_{0}
and
\gamma_{0}
are contained in a
small disk centered at the point
p
. The basepoints
w_{0}
and
z_{0}
are both
contained in this disk, and are in the regions bounded by
\beta_{0}
and
\gamma_{0}
.

Figure 9.1.
The quasi-stabilized Heegaard triple

\mathcal{T}^{+}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\{\alpha_{s}\},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\{\beta_{0}\},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}\cup\{\gamma_{0}\},\bm{\mathbf{w}}\cup\{w_{0}\},\bm{\mathbf{z}}\cup\{z_{0}\})

considered in Proposition
9.7
.

Abusing notation slightly, write

\alpha_{s}\cap\beta_{0}=\{\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}},\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}\},\qquad\alpha_{s}\cap\gamma_{0}=\{\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}},\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}\},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\beta_{0}\cap\gamma_{0}=\{\theta^{+},\theta^{-}\},

where
\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
denotes the top
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
-graded intersection point,
and
\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}
denotes the top
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}
-graded intersection point.
When the relative gradings coincide, we write
\theta^{+}
for the top-graded
intersection point, and
\theta^{-}
for the bottom.

We note that, if
(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a Heegaard triple and

(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\{\alpha_{s}\},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\{\beta_{0}\},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}\cup\{\gamma_{0}\},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a
quasi-stabilization, then the 4-manifolds
X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
and
X_{\alpha\cup\{\alpha_{0}\},\beta\cup\{\beta_{0}\},\gamma\cup\{\gamma_{0}\}}
are canonically diffeomorphic,
since the handlebodies
U_{\alpha}
,
U_{\beta}
, and
U_{\gamma}
are unchanged after we
quasi-stabilize the triple.
In particular,

\Spin^{c}(X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma})\cong\Spin^{c}(X_{\alpha\cup\{\alpha_{0}\},\beta\cup\{\beta_{0}\},\gamma\cup\{\gamma_{0}\}}).

We will need the following holomorphic curve count:

Proposition 9.7
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{T}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a Heegaard link triple,
and
\mathcal{T}^{+}
is its quasi-stabilization. Write
\mathbb{U}
for a doubly-based unknot
containing the basepoints
w_{0}
and
z_{0}
, with Seifert disk
D
,
intersecting
\Sigma
in an arc connecting
w_{0}
and
z_{0}
disjoint
from
\beta_{0}
and
\gamma_{0}
. Then

T_{w_{0},z_{0}}^{+}F_{\mathcal{T},\mathfrak{s}}(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y})=F_{\mathcal{T}^{+}}(T_{w_{0},z_{0}}^{+}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathbb{U},D}(\mathbf{y}))\text{ and }S_{w_{0},z_{0}}^{+}F_{\mathcal{T}}(\mathbf{x}\otimes\mathbf{y})=F_{\mathcal{T}^{+},\mathfrak{s}}(S_{w_{0},z_{0}}^{+}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathbb{U},D}(\mathbf{y})).

Proof.

From the definitions of the maps
S_{w_{0},z_{0}}^{+}
,
T_{w_{0},z_{0}}^{+}
, and

\mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{U},D}^{+}
(see Sections
3.3
and

3.5
), the main claim is equivalent to the claim that

F_{\mathcal{T},\mathfrak{s}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\bm{\mathbf{y}})\times\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}=F_{\mathcal{T}^{+},\mathfrak{s}}\left((\bm{\mathbf{x}}\times\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}})\otimes(\bm{\mathbf{y}}\times\theta^{+})\right)\text{ and }F_{\mathcal{T},\mathfrak{s}}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}\otimes\bm{\mathbf{y}})\times\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}=F_{\mathcal{T}^{+},\mathfrak{s}}\left((\bm{\mathbf{x}}\times\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}})\otimes(\bm{\mathbf{y}}\times\theta^{+})\right),

which is exactly the statement of
[
28
]
*Proposition 6.2.
∎

9.3.
Twisted conjugate Heegaard diagrams for links

Analogous to the proofs of the trace formulas in

[
55
]
and
[
20
]
,
to prove Theorem
9.3
, we define a special kind
of operation on a Heegaard diagram for a link, whose result we will call the

twisted conjugate
of the original. We describe the construction presently.

If
\mathbb{L}=(L,\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a multi-based link, we write
\overline{\mathbb{L}}
for the
multi-based link
(L,\bm{\mathbf{z}},\bm{\mathbf{w}})
, obtained by switching the roles of the basepoints.
Given a Heegaard diagram
\mathcal{H}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
for
(Y,\mathbb{L})
, we can
obtain a diagram for
(Y,\overline{\mathbb{L}})
by reversing the orientation of

\Sigma
, and switching the roles of
\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}
and
\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}
. The resulting diagram

\bar{\mathcal{H}}:=(\bar{\Sigma},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{z}},\bm{\mathbf{w}})
is referred to as the

conjugate
of
\mathcal{H}
; see
[
38
]
*Section 2.2 and

[
13
]
*Section 6.1.

To obtain a diagram for
(Y,\mathbb{L})
, we can modify the embedding of

\bar{\Sigma}
in a neighborhood of
L
. By isotoping
\bar{\Sigma}
along

L
in the positive direction according to the orientation of
L
, we
obtain the
positive twisted conjugate
diagram
\mathit{Tw}^{+}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})
.
Analogously, if we twist in the negative direction, we obtain the

negative twisted conjugate

\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})
. The diagrams

\mathit{Tw}^{+}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})
and
\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})
are illustrated in
Figure
9.2
. We write
\mathit{Tw}^{+}(\bar{\Sigma})
and

\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\Sigma})
for the underlying Heegaard surfaces of the twisted
conjugate diagrams.

Figure 9.2.
On top, we show a link diagram
\mathcal{H}
, and its twisted conjugates

\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})
bottom left and
\mathit{Tw}^{+}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})
bottom right.

9.4.
Doubling Heegaard diagrams for links

An additional type of operation on Heegaard diagrams we will encounter in the proof
of Theorem
9.3
is
doubling
.
In the case of links, if
\mathcal{H}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a
diagram for
(Y,\mathbb{L})
, then there are four natural variations of the doubling
procedure, producing four diagrams

(9.2)

D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}),\quad D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\beta}(\mathcal{H}),\quad D^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{H}),\quad\text{and}\quad D^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}_{\beta}(\mathcal{H}).

For our purposes, it will be sufficient to consider any one of the four
diagrams in equation (
9.2
). We focus on

D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{H})
, whose construction we describe presently.

We first construct the underlying Heegaard surface
D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha}(\Sigma)
.
Let
N(\Sigma)=[-1,1]\times\Sigma
denote a regular
neighborhood of
\Sigma
in
Y
. Let
N(\bm{\mathbf{z}})
denote a regular neighborhood
of the basepoints in
\Sigma
, which is a collection of disks, and let us write

\Sigma_{0}:=\Sigma\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{z}}).

We define

D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha,0}(\Sigma):=\partial([-1,0]\times\Sigma_{0}).

We can view
D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha,0}(\Sigma)
as being formed by gluing a copy of

\Sigma\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{z}})
to
\bar{\Sigma}\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{z}})
along their
boundaries. Note that
D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha,0}(\Sigma)\cap L
consists of the original
basepoints
\bm{\mathbf{w}}\subseteq\Sigma
, as well as another collection of basepoints

\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}
, which are the images of
\bm{\mathbf{w}}
on
\bar{\Sigma}
.

We now describe the attaching curves on
D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)
. Let

m=|\bm{\mathbf{w}}|=|\bm{\mathbf{z}}|
. We pick embedded and pairwise disjoint arcs
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{m}

on
\Sigma
, each traveling from a
\bm{\mathbf{z}}
-basepoint to a
\bm{\mathbf{w}}
-basepoint. We
assume further that each basepoint in
\bm{\mathbf{w}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{z}}
is an endpoint of exactly
one
\lambda_{i}
. We assume that the interiors of the
\lambda_{i}
are disjoint from
\bm{\mathbf{w}}\cup\bm{\mathbf{z}}
.

Next, we pick a collection
A
of subarcs of
\partial\Sigma_{0}
, such that each
component of
\partial\Sigma_{0}
contains exactly one subarc. We further require
that
A
be disjoint from each
\lambda_{i}
. Pick a collection
d_{1},\dots,d_{n}
of properly embedded and pairwise disjoint arcs on
\Sigma_{0}
that have both boundary
components on
A
, are disjoint from the
\lambda_{i}
, and such that
they form a basis of
H_{1}(\Sigma_{0},A)
.

By doubling the arcs
d_{1},\dots,d_{n}
across the connected sum tubes onto all
of
D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)
, we obtain
n
pairwise disjoint simple closed
curves
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}
on
D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)

that do not intersect the arcs
\lambda_{i}
. Let us write

\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}}:=\{\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}\}.

We can now define an initial version of the doubled diagram as

D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H}):=(D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma),\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}},\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}),

where
\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}}
is the copy of
\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}
on
\bar{\Sigma}
.

Via an isotopy of
Y
supported in a neighborhood of
L
that fixes

\bm{\mathbf{w}}
, we can move
\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}
to
\bm{\mathbf{z}}
. We let
D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha}(\Sigma)
denote the
Heegaard surface obtained by isotoping
D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha,0}(\Sigma)
in such a
manner. The diagram
D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{H})
is similarly obtained by pushing
forward the attaching curves on
D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{H})
under such an isotopy;
see Figure
9.3
.

Figure 9.3.
The link diagram
\mathcal{H}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is shown top left,
the preliminary double
D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})
top right,
and the double
D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})
at the bottom.
The curves
\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}}
bounding disks in the
\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}
-handlebody of
D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})

(i.e., the region between the two copies of
\Sigma
) are not shown.

A diagram
D_{\beta}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})
can be constructed using a variation of the
above construction, by having
[0,1]\times(\Sigma\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{z}}))
play the
role of the
\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}
-handlebody. Diagrams
D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\mathcal{H})
and

D_{\beta}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(\mathcal{H})
can be defined similarly, by instead adding tubes near
the
\bm{\mathbf{w}}
-basepoints.

We now proceed to show that
D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})
is a valid Heegaard diagram
for
(L,\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
. Note that it is clearly sufficient to show that

D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})
is a valid Heegaard diagram for
(L,\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime})
. To this
end, we prove the following fact about the
\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}}
curves:

Lemma 9.8
.

The curves
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}
are homologically independent in both

H_{1}(D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{w}}))
and
H_{1}(D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}))
.

Proof.

Let
p_{i}
denote the point
\lambda_{i}\cap\partial\Sigma_{0}
, and write

\bm{\mathbf{p}}=\{p_{1},\dots,p_{m}\}
. Since the
\lambda_{i}
, as well as their images
on
\bar{\Sigma}
, are disjoint from the
\delta_{j}
, if follows that

\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}
are homologically independent in

H_{1}(D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{w}}))
if and only if they are
independent in
H_{1}(D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{z}}^{\prime}))
, which in turn
occurs if an only if they are homologically independent in

H_{1}(D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{p}}))
. Noting that

D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{p}})
can be viewed as
\Sigma_{0}
glued
to
\bar{\Sigma}_{0}
along the arcs
A
, we consider the sequence

(9.3)

H_{1}(D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{p}}))\to H_{1}(D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)\setminus N(\bm{\mathbf{p}}),\bar{\Sigma}_{0})\to H_{1}(\Sigma_{0},A).

Here, the first map is induced by inclusion, and the second is the inverse of the
excision isomorphism. Since the curves
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}
are mapped to
d_{1},\dots,d_{n}
by
the composition of the two maps in equation (
9.3
),
which are homologically independent in
H_{1}(\Sigma_{0},A)
, we
conclude that the curves
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}
are also homologically independent.
∎

Lemma
9.8
implies that
\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}}
is a valid set of
attaching curves on
D_{\alpha,0}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma)
, as the following basic lemma
demonstrates:

Lemma 9.9
.

Suppose that
\Sigma_{0}
is a connected surface-with-boundary, and

\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}\subseteq\Sigma_{0}
is a collection of pairwise
disjoint simple closed curves, with
n=g(\Sigma_{0})+|\partial\Sigma_{0}|-1
. Then
each component of
\Sigma_{0}\setminus(\delta_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\delta_{n})
is
planar and contains exactly one component of
\partial\Sigma_{0}
if and only if

\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}
are homologically independent
H_{1}(\Sigma_{0})
.

Proof.

Assume first that each component of
\Sigma_{0}\setminus(\delta_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\delta_{n})
is planar and contains exactly one component of
\partial\Sigma_{0}
. Each curve
\delta_{i}
determines two boundary components of

\Sigma_{0}\setminus(\delta_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\delta_{n})
. A simple
Mayer–Vietoris argument for gluing along these two boundary components shows
that the curves
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}
are homologically independent in

H_{1}(\Sigma_{0})
.

Conversely, suppose that
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}
are homologically
independent in
H_{1}(\Sigma_{0})
. We note that, if any component of

\Sigma_{0}\setminus(\delta_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\delta_{n})
does not contain a
component of
\partial\Sigma_{0}
, then we obtain a non-trivial relation in

H_{1}(\Sigma_{0})
amongst the
\delta_{i}
. Hence each component of

\Sigma_{0}\setminus(\delta_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\delta_{n})
contains at least one
component of
\partial\Sigma_{0}
. If any component
C
of
\Sigma_{0}\setminus(\delta_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\delta_{n})
is non-planar or contains more than one
component of
\partial\Sigma_{0}
, then we can pick a simple closed curve
\delta^{\prime}

in
C
such that
[\delta^{\prime}]
is not in the span of the classes
[\delta_{i}]
,
for
\delta_{i}\subseteq\partial C
. Using a Mayer–Vietoris argument, such a class

[\delta^{\prime}]
remains homologically independent from the classes
[\delta_{i}]

in
H_{1}(\Sigma_{0})
, and is clearly disjoint from the curves

\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{n}
. However, it is easily verified that the maximal
rank of a subspace of
H_{1}(\Sigma_{0})
on which the intersection form

Q_{\Sigma_{0}}
vanishes is
n=g(\Sigma_{0})+|\partial\Sigma_{0}|-1
, so such a curve

\delta^{\prime}
cannot exist, since we would obtain a subspace of rank
n+1
on
which
Q_{\Sigma_{0}}
vanished. Hence each component of
\Sigma_{0}\setminus(\delta_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\delta_{n})
must be planar and contain exactly one
component of
\partial\Sigma_{0}
.
∎

9.5.
Transition maps and doubled Heegaard diagrams

Suppose that
\mathcal{H}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
is a Heegaard diagram for

(Y,\mathbb{L})
. Let
D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})=(D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma),\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}},\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
denote the doubled diagram from
Section
9.4
, and let

\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})=(\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\Sigma}),\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}},\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})

be the negative twisted conjugate from
Section
9.3
. In this section, we describe
compact formulas for the transition maps between the link Floer complexes for

\mathcal{H}
,
D^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{H})
, and
\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})
.

As a first observation, we note that

(D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\Sigma),\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}},\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})

is a multi-pointed diagram for an unlink in

(\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})^{\#g(\Sigma)}
, where each component has
exactly two basepoints. Hence there is a well-defined top-graded generator

[\Theta_{\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}^{+}]\in\mathcal{H\!F\!L}^{-}(D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H}),\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}},\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}},\mathfrak{s}_{0}),

where
\mathfrak{s}_{0}
is the torsion
\Spin^{c}
structure on
(\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})^{\#g(\Sigma)}
.

Lemma 9.10
.

If
\mathcal{H}
is a Heegaard diagram and
D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})
is its double, then

\Phi_{\mathcal{H}\to D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})}(-)\simeq F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\beta},\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}(F_{1}^{\bar{\beta},\bar{\beta}}(-)\otimes\Theta_{\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}^{+}).

Proof.

The key observation is that the map
F_{1}^{\bar{\beta},\bar{\beta}}
is equal to a
composition of 1-handle maps, while

F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\beta},\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}(-\otimes\Theta^{+}_{\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta})

is chain homotopic to the 2-handle map for a collection of 2-handles that cancel
the 1-handles which were added by
F_{1}^{\bar{\beta},\bar{\beta}}
.
See
[
55
]
*Proposition 7.2 for a detailed proof of a closely related result.
∎

Next, we need a simple formula for the transition map from
\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})

to
D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})
. A handle cancellation argument yields the following:

Lemma 9.11
.

There is a chain homotopy

\Phi_{\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}})\to D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})}(-)\simeq F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\beta},\alpha\cup\bar{\alpha},\Delta}(F_{1}^{\alpha,\alpha}(-)\otimes\Theta^{+}_{\alpha\cup\bar{\alpha},\Delta}).

Finally, we describe a formula for the transition map from

D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})
to
\mathcal{H}
, which is essentially just the dual of
Lemma
9.10
. Suppose
D_{1},\dots,D_{n}
are
compressing disks attached to the
\bar{\Sigma}
portion of

\Sigma\#_{i}\bar{\Sigma}
that bound the curves in
\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}}
. If we surger

\Sigma\#_{i}\bar{\Sigma}
along the
\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}}
curves using the compressing disks

D_{1},\dots,D_{n}
, we simply obtain the original Heegaard surface
\Sigma
(up
to isotopy, relative to
L\cap\Sigma
). With this in mind, the handle
cancellation argument used to prove Lemma
9.10

implies the following:

Lemma 9.12
.

There is a chain homotopy

\Phi_{D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H})\to\mathcal{H}}(-)\simeq F_{3}^{\bar{\beta},\bar{\beta}}F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta,\beta\cup\bar{\beta}}(-\otimes\Theta_{\Delta,\beta\cup\bar{\beta}}^{+}).

9.6.
Intertwining maps and connected sums

Suppose that
(Y_{j},\mathbb{L}_{j})
for
j\in\{1,2\}
is a 3-manifolds with a
multi-based link, and
\mathcal{H}_{j}=(\Sigma_{j},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{j},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{j},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{j},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{j})

is a Heegaard diagram for
(Y_{j},\mathbb{L}_{j})
. Suppose also we have chosen a bijection
i
from

\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{1}
to
\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{2}
. We can form the
generalized connected sum
of

(Y_{1},\mathbb{L}_{1})
and
(Y_{2},\mathbb{L}_{2})
, for which we write

(Y_{1}\#_{i}Y_{2},\mathbb{L}_{1}\#_{i}\mathbb{L}_{2})
, by deleting 3-balls centered at each point in
\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{1}
and
\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{2}
,
and gluing the boundary components according to our chosen bijection
between
\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{1}
and
\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{2}
. The link
\mathbb{L}_{1}\#_{i}\mathbb{L}_{2}
is decorated with the
basepoints
\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{1}
and
\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{2}
.

We can construct a Heegaard surface
\Sigma_{1}\#_{i}\Sigma_{2}
for
(Y_{1}\#_{i}Y_{2},\mathbb{L}_{1}\#_{i}\mathbb{L}_{2})
by adding a connected sum tube between
\Sigma_{1}
and

\Sigma_{2}
near each basepoint in
\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{1}
and the corresponding basepoint in

\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{2}
. We define

\mathcal{H}_{1}\#_{i}\mathcal{H}_{2}:=(\Sigma_{1}\#_{i}\Sigma_{2},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{1}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{2},\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{1},\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{2})

for the resulting diagram.

Adapting the construction from
[
38
]
*Section 6.2, we can define an
intertwining map

\mathcal{G}\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathfrak{s}_{1})\otimes_{\mathcal{R}^{\infty}}\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{2},\mathfrak{s}_{2})\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{1}\#_{i}\mathcal{H}_{2},\mathfrak{s}_{1}\#\mathfrak{s}_{2}),

via the formula

(9.4)

\mathcal{G}(-,-):=F_{\alpha_{1}\cup\alpha_{2},\beta_{1}\cup\alpha_{2},\beta_{1}\cup\beta_{2}}(F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}(-)).

We now show that the map
\mathcal{G}
is chain homotopic to a link cobordism map. We
define the decorated link cobordism
(W,\mathcal{F})
, as follows. Write

\bm{\mathbf{z}}_{1}=\{z_{1,1},\dots,z_{1,n}\}
and
\bm{\mathbf{w}}_{2}=\{w_{2,1},\dots,w_{2,n}\}
,
ordered such that
z_{1,i}
and
w_{2,i}
are paired. The 4-manifold
W
is
obtained by attaching
n
1-handles, such that the
i^{\text{th}}
1-handle
has one foot at
z_{1,i}
, and the other foot at
w_{2,i}
. We construct a
surface
S
inside the 1-handle cobordism by attaching a band inside each
1-handle. We construct a dividing set
\mathcal{A}\subseteq S
as follows. For each
pair
(z_{1,i},w_{2,i})
we add a dividing arc
a_{i}
which has one end on

Y_{1}
, and the other on
Y_{2}
, and travels through the 1-handle connecting

z_{1,i}
to
w_{2,i}
. One end of
a_{i}
occurs immediately after
z_{1,i}
,
and the other end occurs immediately after
w_{2,i}
. The remaining arcs of

\mathcal{A}
are of the form
I\times\{p\}
, for points
p\in L_{1}\cup L_{2}
. We
define
\mathcal{F}:=(S,\mathcal{A})
; see Figure
9.4
.

Figure 9.4.
The decorated link cobordism used to define the map
G
,
when
\mathbb{L}_{1}
and
\mathbb{L}_{2}
are both doubly-based knots. The orientation of
\mathbb{L}_{1}
and
\mathbb{L}_{2}
is shown.

We define the cobordism map

G:=F_{W,\mathcal{F}}.

In the case when
\mathbb{L}_{1}
and
\mathbb{L}_{2}
are doubly-based knots,
using the decomposition shown in Figure
9.4
, we see

(9.5)

G\simeq S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{-}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}F_{1},

where
F_{1}
denotes the 1-handle map. More generally, if
\mathbb{L}_{1}
and
\mathbb{L}_{2}

have many basepoints, the cobordism map
G
is a composition of
n
terms
which each have the form shown in equation (
9.5
).

Note that there is an asymmetry between
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
in the definition of

G
. At each pair of basepoints we delete, we could instead do a type-
\bm{\mathbf{z}}

band map, followed by the
T_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{-}
quasi-stabilization map. The
corresponding decorated link cobordism is not diffeomorphic to
(W,\mathcal{F})

(they can be distinguished by looking at the order in which the boundary
components appear on the subsurface
S_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
, with respect to the boundary
orientation). There is a similar asymmetry also in the definition of
\mathcal{G}

since the formula defining
\mathcal{G}
is not invariant under switching the roles
of
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
.

Proposition 9.13
.

The intertwining map
\mathcal{G}
is chain homotopic to the link cobordism map
G
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of

[
55
]
*Proposition 8.1. The idea is that we
exhibit a chain homotopy inverse of
G
, which we denote
E
, and show that

(9.6)

E\circ\mathcal{G}\simeq\id.

For notational simplicity, we restrict to the case when
\mathbb{L}_{1}
and
\mathbb{L}_{2}

are both doubly-based knots. The proof we present extends to the more
general case by an elaboration of notation.

We define the map
E
via the formula

(9.7)

E:=F_{3}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}.

We note
E
is the cobordism map for the decorated link cobordism obtained by
turning around and reversing the orientation of the link cobordism used to
define
G
. The fact that
E
and
G
are chain homotopy inverses of each
other follows from
[
58
]
*Proposition 5.1.
Using equation (
9.7
), we see equation (
9.6
) is equivalent to

(9.8)

F_{3}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{\alpha_{1}\cup\alpha_{2},\beta_{1}\cup\alpha_{2},\beta_{1}\cup\beta_{2}}(F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}\otimes F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}})\simeq\id.

We pick two curves in the connected sum region of
\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2}
,
which we label as
\xi_{s}
and
\zeta_{0}
. The curve
\zeta_{0}
bounds a small
disk containing the basepoints
w_{2}
and
z_{1}
, while
\xi_{s}
wraps all the
way around the connected sum tube; see Figure
9.5
. We write

\xi_{s}\cap\zeta_{0}=\{\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}_{\xi_{s},\zeta_{0}},\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\xi_{s},\zeta_{0}}\},

where
\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}_{\xi_{s},\zeta_{0}}
is the top
\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
-graded
intersection point and
\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\xi_{s},\zeta_{0}}
denotes the top

\gr_{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}
-graded intersection point.

Figure 9.5.
The connected sum region of
\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2}
.

The map
S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}
appearing in equation (
9.8
) is defined by the equation

S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}):=\bm{\mathbf{x}}\times\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}_{\xi_{s},\zeta_{0}},

extended
\mathcal{R}^{\infty}
-equivariantly. Similarly, there is a birth map
(corresponding to the cobordism map for a doubly-based unknot being born),
given by the formula

\mathcal{B}^{+}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}):=\bm{\mathbf{x}}\times\theta^{+}_{\zeta_{0},\zeta_{0}},

where
\theta^{+}_{\zeta_{0},\zeta_{0}}
denotes the top-graded intersection point
of
\zeta_{0}
and a small Hamiltonian translate of
\zeta_{0}
. Note that, to
ease the notational burden, we will henceforth not distinguish between a
curve and its Hamiltonian translate (though, implicitly, when we are counting
holomorphic triangles, we must translate some of the curves using
Hamiltonians).

We introduce the following shorthand notation for sets of attaching curves on
\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2}
:

L:=\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{1}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2},\qquad M:=\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2},\qquad\text{and}\qquad R:=\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{2}.

We define

L_{0}:=\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{1}\cup\{\zeta_{0}\}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad L_{s}:=\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{1}\cup\{\xi_{s}\}\cup\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2},

and we define
M_{0}
,
M_{s}
,
R_{0}
, and
R_{s}
similarly.

By Proposition
9.7
,

(9.9)

\begin{split}&S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{L,M,R}(F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}(-))\simeq\\
&F_{L_{s},M_{0},R_{0}}(S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes\mathcal{B}^{+}F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}(-)).\end{split}

The band map
F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
is defined via the triangle count

(9.10)

F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(-):=F_{L_{s},R_{0},R_{s}}(-\otimes(\Theta^{+}_{R,R}\times\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\zeta_{0},\xi_{s}})).

We note that

\Theta^{+}_{R,R}\times\theta^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}_{\zeta_{0},\xi_{s}}=T_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{+}_{R,R}),

by definition, so equation (
9.10
) reads

(9.11)

F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(-)=F_{L_{s},R_{0},R_{s}}(-\otimes T_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{+}_{R,R})).

Combining equations (
9.9
) and (
9.11
),
and using associativity, we see that

(9.12)

\begin{split}&F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}\mathcal{G}(-,-)\simeq\\
&F_{L_{s},R_{0},R_{s}}\left(F_{L_{s},M_{0},R_{0}}\left(S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes\mathcal{B}^{+}F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}(-)\right)\otimes T_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{+}_{R,R})\right)\simeq\\
&F_{L_{s},M_{0},R_{s}}\left(S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes F_{M_{0},R_{0},R_{s}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{+}F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}(-)\otimes T_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}(\Theta^{+}_{R,R})\right)\right).\end{split}

Using Proposition
9.7
, equation (
9.12
) becomes

(9.13)

F_{L_{s},M_{0},R_{s}}\left(S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes T_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{M,R,R}\left(F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}(-)\otimes\Theta^{+}_{R,R}\right)\right).

The expression
F_{M,R,R}(-\otimes\Theta^{+}_{R,R})
is the
change of diagrams map for shifting the curves
R
slightly, which we can
safely delete, since we are already precomposing with a change of diagrams
map on
\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(Y_{1},\mathbb{L}_{1},\mathfrak{s}_{1})\otimes\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(Y_{2},\mathbb{L}_{2},\mathfrak{s}_{2})
.
Hence equation (
9.13
) becomes

(9.14)

F_{L_{s},M_{0},R_{s}}(S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes T_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}(-)).

Define the map

\Top_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}^{+}\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{2},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{2})\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{1},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1})\otimes\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{2},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{2})

via the formula

\Top^{+}_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}(\bm{\mathbf{x}}):=\Theta^{+}_{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}\otimes\bm{\mathbf{x}},

extended
\mathcal{R}^{\infty}
-equivariantly. Next, we claim

(9.15)

T^{+}_{z_{1},w_{2}}F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}(-)\simeq F_{B^{\prime}}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}F_{1}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}\Top^{+}_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}(-),

where

F_{B^{\prime}}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2},M_{s},R_{s})\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma_{1}\#\Sigma_{2},M_{0},R_{s})

is the band map

(9.16)

F_{B^{\prime}}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}(-):=F_{M_{0},M_{s},R_{s}}(T_{z_{1},w_{2}}^{+}(\Theta_{M,M}^{+})\otimes-).

Note that
F_{B^{\prime}}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
is an
\alpha
-band map, because the handlebody

U_{M_{0}}
is playing the role of the
\alpha
-handlebody. Furthermore, the
quasi-stabilization map
T_{z_{1},w_{2}}^{+}
appears in
equation (
9.16
) instead of
T_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}
because

U_{M_{0}}
is now playing the role of the
\alpha
-handlebody instead of the

\beta
-handlebody, so the induced orientation of the strands it contains are
reversed, and hence, in this handlebody,
z_{1}
now immediately follows
w_{2}
.

It is possible to establish equation (
9.15
) via a
direct holomorphic triangle count. Indeed, by using
Proposition
9.5
, one could delete the
portion added via the compound 1-handle map, and reduce the computation to a
holomorphic triangle count supported in a disk, involving three isotopic
attaching curves. A holomorphic triangle count could then be performed by
using a neck-stretching argument, as in
[
58
]
*Lemma 8.6.

A more conceptually enlightening approach for proving
equation (
9.15
), and the approach we take, is to
interpret the maps appearing as cobordism maps and use properties of the link Floer TQFT. We note that the map

\Top_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}^{+}
can be written as the composition of a single 0-handle map, followed by
|\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1}|
1-handle maps.
After rearranging handles and canceling the 0-handle added by
\Top^{+}_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}

with the 1-handle added by
F_{1}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}
,
the composition
F_{1}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}\Top^{+}_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}

can be rewritten as the composition of the cobordism map
F_{1}
induced by attaching

|\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1}|
1-handles to
Y_{2}
, followed by a birth cobordism map, which adds the
doubly-based knot
\mathbb{U}=(U,w_{1},z_{1})
. Hence, we can write

(9.17)

F_{1}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}\Top^{+}_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}\simeq\mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{U}}^{+}F_{1}.

Similarly,

(9.18)

F_{1}^{\beta_{1},\beta_{1}}\simeq\phi_{*}F_{1},

where
\phi
is an isotopy of
Y_{2}\#(\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})^{\#|\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1}|}
that moves the knot in
Y_{2}
into the 1-handle region formed when
we attach
(\Sigma_{1},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1})
.

We can decompose the isotopy
\phi
as the composition of an isotopy

\phi_{0}
, which fixes
w_{2}
and
z_{2}
but moves the link, followed by an
isotopy
\tau^{w_{1}\leftarrow w_{2}}
that fixes the link setwise, is supported in
a neighborhood of the link, fixes
z_{2}
, but moves
w_{2}
to
w_{1}
. Using
equations (
9.17
) and (
9.18
),
we see that equation (
9.15
) is equivalent to

(9.19)

T_{z_{1},w_{2}}^{+}\tau^{w_{1}\leftarrow w_{2}}_{*}(\phi_{0})_{*}F_{1}\simeq F_{B^{\prime}}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathbb{U}}F_{1}.

We now simply note that equation (
3.10
) implies

T_{z_{1},w_{2}}^{+}\tau^{w_{1}\leftarrow w_{2}}_{*}\simeq T_{w_{1},z_{1}}^{+}
, while
equation (
3.16
) implies
F_{B^{\prime}}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}\mathcal{B}_{\mathbb{U}}^{+}\simeq T_{w_{1},z_{1}}^{+}(\phi_{0})_{*}
. Together, these establish
equation (
9.19
), and hence also equation (
9.15
).

If we substitute the formula (
9.16
) for
F_{B^{\prime}}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}

into equation (
9.15
), equation (
9.14
) becomes

(9.20)

F_{L_{s},M_{0},R_{s}}\left(S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes F_{M_{0},M_{s},R_{s}}\left(T_{z_{1},w_{2}}^{+}(\Theta_{M,M}^{+})\otimes F_{1}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}\Top^{+}_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}(-)\right)\right).

By associativity, we see that equation (
9.20
) is chain homotopic to

(9.21)

F_{L_{s},M_{s},R_{s}}\left(F_{L_{s},M_{0},M_{s}}\left(S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes T_{z_{1},w_{2}}^{+}(\Theta_{M,M}^{+})\right)\otimes F_{1}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}\Top^{+}_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}(-)\right)

After post-composing equation (
9.21
) with the
3-handle map
F_{3}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}
, and pulling the 3-handle map inside
the outer triangle map using Proposition
9.5
, we obtain that the
composition
E\circ\mathcal{G}
is chain homotopic to

(9.22)

F_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\sqcup\mathcal{T}_{2}}\left(F_{3}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}F_{L_{s},M_{0},M_{s}}\left(S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes T_{z_{1},w_{2}}^{+}(\Theta_{M,M}^{+})\right)\otimes\Top^{+}_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}(-)\right).

where
\mathcal{T}_{1}\sqcup\mathcal{T}_{2}
denotes the disjoint union of the Heegaard
triples
(\Sigma_{1},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{1},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{1})
and
(\Sigma_{2},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}_{2})
.
Since the outer triangle map is on the disjoint union of
\Sigma_{1}
and

\Sigma_{2}
, we direct our attention to the inner triangle map. We claim that

(9.23)

F_{3}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}F_{L_{s},M_{0},M_{s}}\left(S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}(-)\otimes T_{z_{1},w_{2}}^{+}(\Theta_{M,M}^{+})\right)\simeq\Top_{(\Sigma_{2},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2})}^{+}.

Note that, by the definition of
F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}
, the left-hand side of equation (
9.23
) is

(9.24)

F_{3}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}.

Our strategy for proving equation (
9.23
) will be to
manipulate equation (
9.24
) using algebraic
properties of the TQFT until it becomes the cobordism map for the disjoint
union of the identity cobordism
I\times Y_{1}
, and a 4-dimensional handlebody
bounding
Y_{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}
.

We can write
F_{1}^{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}
as
(\phi_{*})F_{1}
, where
F_{1}
is a 1-handle
cobordism, and
\phi
is a diffeomorphism that moves a small portion of the
link near
z_{1}
into
Y_{\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2}}
, and sends
z_{1}
to
z_{2}
. Note that we
can write
\phi
as a composition
\rho^{z_{1}\to z_{2}}\circ\phi_{0}
, where

\phi_{0}
moves a small portion of
K_{1}
near
z_{1}
, but fixes
z_{1}
, and

\rho^{z_{1}\to z_{2}}
is a diffeomorphism that is fixed outside a neighborhood
of the subarc of
\phi_{0}(K_{1})
containing
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
, but sends
z_{1}
to

z_{2}
. We note that the map
\rho^{z_{1}\to z_{2}}_{*}
satisfies the relation

(9.25)

\rho^{z_{1}\to z_{2}}_{*}\simeq S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{-}T_{z_{2},w_{2}}^{+},

by
[
58
]
*Lemma 4.25; cf. equation (
3.11
) and Figure
3.6
.

We perform the following manipulation:

(9.26)

\displaystyle\phi_{*}

\displaystyle\simeq\rho^{z_{1}\to z_{2}}_{*}(\phi_{0})_{*}

\displaystyle\simeq S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{-}T_{z_{2},w_{2}}^{+}(\phi_{0})_{*}

\displaystyle(\text{equation~\eqref{eq:basepointmovingjustz}})

\displaystyle\simeq S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{-}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathbb{U}}

\displaystyle(\text{equation~\eqref{eq:band-birth=quasi2}}),

where
\mathbb{U}
denotes
(U,w_{2},z_{2})
. Hence equation (
9.24
) becomes

(9.27)

F_{3}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{-}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathbb{U}}F_{1}.

We note that the 1-handles of
F_{1}
can be moved to the left of all the other
maps. After moving
F_{1}
to the left, the birth cobordism map
\mathcal{B}^{+}_{\mathbb{U}}

becomes the composition of a 0-handle map
F_{0}
(which adds a 4-ball
containing
\mathbb{U}
), and the 1-handle map
F_{1}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}
. We also note
that
S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}S_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{-}\simeq\Phi_{w_{2}}
by equation (
3.8
).
Hence equation (
9.27
) becomes

(9.28)

F_{1}^{\prime}F_{3}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}\Phi_{w_{2}}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{w}}}F_{1}^{\xi_{s},\xi_{s}}F_{0},

where
F_{1}^{\prime}
denotes the cobordism map for attaching
|\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2}|
1-handles to the 3-sphere added by
F_{0}
. Using equation (
3.19
), we can reduce equation (
9.28
) to the expression

F_{1}^{\prime}F_{0},

which is clearly just
\Top^{+}_{(\Sigma_{2},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2})}
, establishing equation (
9.23
).

Applying the relation from equation (
9.23
) to equation (
9.22
),
it follows that
E\circ\mathcal{G}
is chain homotopic to

F_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\sqcup\mathcal{T}_{2}}\left(\Top_{(\Sigma_{2},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{2})}^{+}(-)\otimes\Top_{(\Sigma_{1},\beta_{1},\beta_{1})}^{+}(-)\right).

This holomorphic triangle count appears on the disjoint union of
\Sigma_{1}

and
\Sigma_{2}
, and is clearly just the tensor product
\Phi_{\beta_{1}\to\beta_{1}}^{\alpha_{1}}\otimes\Phi_{\beta_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}\to\alpha_{2}}
, completing the proof.
∎

Remark 9.14
.

There is another chain homotopy equivalence
E^{\prime}
from the connected sum to
the disjoint union, defined via the formula
E^{\prime}:=F_{3}F_{B}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}T_{w_{2},z_{1}}^{+}
.
The map
E^{\prime}
corresponds to a pair-of-pants link cobordism where the
type-
\bm{\mathbf{w}}
and type-
\bm{\mathbf{z}}
regions have been switched from the cobordism
corresponding to
E
. One might expect the above argument to also go through
using
E^{\prime}
to try to cancel
\mathcal{G}
, by just replacing each
type-
T
quasi-stabilization map with a type-
S
quasi-stabilization map, and
replacing each type-
\bm{\mathbf{w}}
band map with a type-
\bm{\mathbf{z}}
band map. However, the
careful reader will discover that such a strategy fails at equation (
9.15
).

9.7.
Proof of the triangle cobordism formula

We now prove that the cobordism map induced by

(X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma})
is chain homotopic to the holomorphic
triangle map:

Proof of Theorem
9.3
.

A handlebody description of the 4-manifold
X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
is given in the
proof of
[
55
]
*Theorem 9.1. Let
f_{\beta}
denote a
Morse function on the handlebody
U_{\beta}
compatible with the attaching curves

\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\subseteq\Sigma
, which has
\Sigma
as a maximal level set.
The 4-manifold
X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
has the following handlebody description:

•

A 1-handle for each index 0 critical point of
f_{\beta}
, with one foot at the critical point in

\bar{U}_{\beta}\subseteq Y_{\alpha,\beta}
, and the other foot at its image in
U_{\beta}\subseteq Y_{\beta,\gamma}
.

•

A 2-handle for each index 1 critical point of
f_{\beta}
. The attaching
sphere is equal to the union of the corresponding descending manifold in

U_{\beta}
, concatenated across the connected sum tubes with its mirror image
in
\bar{U}_{\beta}
. There is a canonical framing specified by taking an
arbitrary framing in the portion in
U_{\beta}
, and mirroring it in the portion
in
\bar{U}_{\beta}
.

Let
W_{1}
denote the 1-handle cobordism, and let
W_{2}
denote the 2-handle
cobordism. Let
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
denote the intersection of the decorated surface

\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
with
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
, respectively. Note that
\mathcal{F}_{1}
is obtained by
attaching a collection of bands, one for each 1-handle, each containing a
single dividing arc that meets both
Y_{\alpha,\beta}
and
Y_{\beta,\gamma}
. Hence, we
can write

(9.29)

F_{X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}}\simeq F_{W_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2}}\circ F_{W_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1}}.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{\alpha,\beta}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
,
and let
\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\beta,\gamma})=(\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\Sigma}),\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}},\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})

denote the negative twisted conjugate of the diagram

\mathcal{H}_{\beta,\gamma}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\beta}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
described in Section
9.3
.
To show the main claim, we need to compute the cobordism map starting at the
diagram
\mathcal{H}_{\alpha,\beta}\sqcup\mathcal{H}_{\beta,\gamma}
. However, for the computation, it is
more convenient to start at the diagram
\mathcal{H}_{\alpha,\beta}\sqcup\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\beta,\gamma})
. Hence, we precompose with the change of diagrams
map
\id\otimes\Phi_{\mathcal{H}_{\beta,\gamma}\to\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\beta,\gamma})}
. To simplify
notation, we will omit writing this change of diagrams map for most of the
proof, though it will reappear at the end.

We note that, by Proposition
9.13
,

(9.30)

F_{W_{1},\mathcal{F}_{1}}\simeq\mathcal{G},

where

\mathcal{G}\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\alpha,\beta})\otimes\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\beta,\gamma}))\to\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(\Sigma\#_{i}\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\Sigma}),\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})

is the intertwining map defined by the equation

\mathcal{G}(-,-):=F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\beta}}(F_{1}^{\bar{\gamma},\bar{\gamma}}(-)\otimes F_{1}^{\beta,\beta}(-)).

Hence

(9.31)

F_{X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}}\simeq F_{W_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2}}\circ\mathcal{G}.

Next, it is not hard to see that the Heegaard triple
(\Sigma\#_{i}\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\Sigma}),\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}\cup\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}}},\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}\cup\bar{\bm{\mathbf{\beta}}},\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
can
be used to compute the 2-handle cobordism map
F_{W_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2}}
,
where
\bm{\mathbf{\Delta}}
was defined in Section
9.4
; see

[
55
]
*Lemma 7.7 for a detailed argument in a very
closely related context. It follows that

F_{W_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2}}\simeq F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}(-\otimes\Theta_{\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}^{+}).

Hence, omitting the initial factor of
\id\otimes\Phi_{\mathcal{H}_{\beta,\gamma}\to\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\beta,\gamma})}
,
we have, by associativity,

(9.32)

\begin{split}F_{X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}}(-,-)&\simeq(F_{W_{2},\mathcal{F}_{2}}\circ\mathcal{G})(-,-)\\
&\simeq F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}\left(F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\beta}}\left(F_{1}^{\bar{\gamma},\bar{\gamma}}(-)\otimes F_{1}^{\beta,\beta}(-)\right)\otimes\Theta_{\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}^{+}\right)\\
&\simeq F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\Delta}\left(F_{1}^{\bar{\gamma},\bar{\gamma}}(-)\otimes F_{\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}\left(F_{1}^{\beta,\beta}(-)\otimes\Theta_{\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}^{+}\right)\right).\end{split}

It is not hard to see that the Heegaard diagram corresponding to the codomain
of the map in equation (
9.32
) is the double

D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\alpha,\gamma})
of the diagram
\mathcal{H}_{\alpha,\gamma}=(\Sigma,\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}},\bm{\mathbf{\gamma}},\bm{\mathbf{w}},\bm{\mathbf{z}})
,
so we must postcompose with the transition map

\Phi_{D_{\alpha}^{\bm{\mathbf{z}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\alpha,\gamma})\to\mathcal{H}_{\alpha,\gamma}}
, which we computed in
Lemma
9.12
. Accordingly, our expression from
equation (
9.32
) for
F_{X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}}
becomes

(9.33)

F_{3}^{\bar{\gamma},\bar{\gamma}}F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\gamma},\Delta,\gamma\cup\bar{\gamma}}\left(F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\Delta}\left(F_{1}^{\bar{\gamma},\bar{\gamma}}(-)\otimes F_{\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}\left(F_{1}^{\beta,\beta}(-)\otimes\Theta_{\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}^{+}\right)\right)\otimes\Theta^{+}_{\Delta,\gamma\cup\bar{\gamma}}\right).

Associativity implies that equation (
9.33
) is chain homotopic to

(9.34)

F_{3}^{\bar{\gamma},\bar{\gamma}}F_{\alpha\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\gamma\cup\bar{\gamma}}\left(F_{1}^{\bar{\gamma},\bar{\gamma}}(-)\otimes F_{\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\Delta,\gamma\cup\bar{\gamma}}\left(F_{\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}\left(F_{1}^{\beta,\beta}(-)\otimes\Theta_{\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}^{+}\right)\otimes\Theta^{+}_{\Delta,\gamma\cup\bar{\gamma}}\right)\right).

Using Proposition
9.5
,
we see equation (
9.34
) is chain homotopic to

(9.35)

F_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\left(-\otimes F_{3}^{\bar{\gamma},\bar{\gamma}}\left(F_{\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\Delta,\gamma\cup\bar{\gamma}}\left(F_{\beta\cup\bar{\gamma},\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}\left(F_{1}^{\beta,\beta}(-)\otimes\Theta_{\beta\cup\bar{\beta},\Delta}^{+}\right)\otimes\Theta_{\Delta,\gamma\cup\bar{\gamma}}^{+}\right)\right)\right).

Lemmas
9.11
and
9.12

imply that equation (
9.35
) is chain homotopic to

F_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}(-\otimes\Phi_{\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\bar{\mathcal{H}}_{\beta,\gamma})\to\mathcal{H}_{\beta,\gamma}}).

The transition map inside the triangle map cancels the initial factor of

\id\otimes\Phi_{\mathcal{H}_{\beta,\gamma}\to\mathit{Tw}^{-}(\mathcal{H}_{\beta,\gamma})}
, which we have been
omitting writing up until now. Hence
F_{X_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma},\mathcal{F}_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}}\simeq F_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}
, concluding the proof.
∎

10.
Examples

In this section, we perform some model computations to illustrate our
invariants defined in Section
4
for pairs of slice disks.
Our two main examples will be slice disks constructed by roll-spinning,
and deform-spinning using the rigid motion deformation from Section
8
.
See Section
2.1
for the definitions of roll-spinning and deform-spinning.

10.1.
Invariants of deform-spun slice disks

As stated in the introduction, our main computational
results rely on a formula for the fundamental principal invariants of
deform-spun slice disks, generalizing
[
21
, Theorem 5.1]

from the hat to the full infinity version of knot Floer homology:

Theorem
1.2
.

Let
D_{K,\varphi}
be a slice disk of the knot
-K\#K
, obtained by deform-spinning
a knot
K
in
\mathbb{S}^{3}
using an automorphism
\varphi
of
(\mathbb{S}^{3},K)
.
Let
w
and
z
be basepoints on
K
, and write
C:=\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(K,w,z)
. Then

E\circ\bm{\mathbf{t}}_{D_{K,\varphi}}^{\infty}\simeq(\id\otimes\varphi_{*})\circ\cotr\in\Hom_{\mathcal{R}^{\infty}}(\mathcal{R}^{\infty},C^{\vee}\otimes C),

where the chain homotopy equivalence
E\colon\mathcal{C\!F\!L}^{\infty}(-K\#K,w,z)\to C^{\vee}\otimes C
is described
in
[
56
]
